What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Yeah… thanks for seeing me. I guess it’s maybe not a huge dramatic thing, but I’ve just kind of stopped caring about the stuff I used to look forward to. My fishing gear is sitting in the garage untouched, and my woodshop… it feels more like a chore than something I want to do. Even when my kids ask me to play chess, I do it, but I’m mostly just going through the motions. I don’t know if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Did this start after a specific stressful event or major life change?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess it did, sort of. It’s been going on for about a year and seven months now. Around when my wife’s health problems started changing everything at home. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m blaming that, you know, but that’s about when I started noticing I wasn’t really myself.\n\nDoctor: For most of the past two years, have you felt down or depressed more days than not?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to judge “most,” honestly. I mean, I wouldn’t say I’m down every single day… but yeah, it’s been there a lot. More days than I’d like to admit, I guess. I’ve tried to just keep moving and not make a big thing of it, but it’s been pretty frequent.\n\nDoctor: Have these down feelings lasted continuously for at least two years?\n\nPatient: Not quite two years, no. I mean, it feels like it’s been a long time, but if I’m counting right it’s more like a year and seven months… since my wife’s health stuff started changing things at home. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: If your wife’s health problems had not happened, do you think you would still be feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know for sure… I’d like to think maybe I wouldn’t be. It really seemed to start when my wife’s health problems changed everything at home. That was about a year and seven months ago, and that’s when I started feeling like this. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel your reaction to your wife’s health problems has been stronger than most people would have in the same situation?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean, it’s a hard situation, so I don’t really know what a “normal” reaction would look like. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m handling it worse than someone else would… but it has hit me pretty hard. I guess I’m not the best judge of whether it’s more than most people.\n\nDoctor: During this time, have you felt depressed or down most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to measure “most of the day,” honestly. There have definitely been stretches where I’ve felt pretty down for a while, but I don’t want to overstate it or say it wrong. I guess I’d say it’s happened in periods, yeah… but I’m not sure if it meets exactly what you mean by nearly every day for two weeks.\n\nDoctor: Has there been a period of at least two weeks when you felt down or depressed nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I can say it that clearly. There have been times where it felt like I was down a lot of the time, maybe most days, but I’m not sure I tracked it well enough to say “yes, definitely two weeks nearly every day.” I don’t want to give you the wrong answer.\n\nDoctor: During this past year and seven months, was there ever a stretch when you clearly felt depressed nearly every day for two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I know you’re trying to get a clear yes or no. Looking back, there were definitely stretches where I was really low most of the time, but saying it was nearly every day for two straight weeks—I don’t know if I can honestly pin it down that clearly.\n\nI don’t want to dodge the question. I just… I’d have to say I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: During any 2-week period in the past year and seven months, did you feel sad, down, or hopeless most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I know I’m probably not giving you the clean answer you need. I just… I don’t know if I can say “yes” to that with confidence. There were times I felt really down, more than I’d like to admit, but whether it was most of the day nearly every day for a full two weeks… I’m not sure.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I’d like to think if she were better and things calmed down at home, I’d bounce back. I mean, that sounds like the right answer.

But… honestly, I’m not sure. The loss of interest feels like it’s gotten pretty broad. Like, my fishing gear is just sitting untouched in the garage, and I used to really look forward to that. My woodshop feels more like a chore now than something I enjoy. Even when my kids ask me to play chess, I’ll do it, but I’m kind of just going through the motions.

So… I don’t know. Maybe things being normal would help, but I’m not sure it would all just come back right away.

I don’t know if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Did this start after a specific stressful event or major life change?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess it did, sort of. It’s been going on for about a year and seven months now. Around when my wife’s health problems started changing everything at home. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m blaming that, you know, but that’s about when I started noticing I wasn’t really myself.\n\nDoctor: For most of the past two years, have you felt down or depressed more days than not?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to judge “most,” honestly. I mean, I wouldn’t say I’m down every single day… but yeah, it’s been there a lot. More days than I’d like to admit, I guess. I’ve tried to just keep moving and not make a big thing of it, but it’s been pretty frequent.\n\nDoctor: Have these down feelings lasted continuously for at least two years?\n\nPatient: Not quite two years, no. I mean, it feels like it’s been a long time, but if I’m counting right it’s more like a year and seven months… since my wife’s health stuff started changing things at home. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: If your wife’s health problems had not happened, do you think you would still be feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know for sure… I’d like to think maybe I wouldn’t be. It really seemed to start when my wife’s health problems changed everything at home. That was about a year and seven months ago, and that’s when I started feeling like this. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel your reaction to your wife’s health problems has been stronger than most people would have in the same situation?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean, it’s a hard situation, so I don’t really know what a “normal” reaction would look like. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m handling it worse than someone else would… but it has hit me pretty hard. I guess I’m not the best judge of whether it’s more than most people.\n\nDoctor: During this time, have you felt depressed or down most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to measure “most of the day,” honestly. There have definitely been stretches where I’ve felt pretty down for a while, but I don’t want to overstate it or say it wrong. I guess I’d say it’s happened in periods, yeah… but I’m not sure if it meets exactly what you mean by nearly every day for two weeks.\n\nDoctor: Has there been a period of at least two weeks when you felt down or depressed nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I can say it that clearly. There have been times where it felt like I was down a lot of the time, maybe most days, but I’m not sure I tracked it well enough to say “yes, definitely two weeks nearly every day.” I don’t want to give you the wrong answer.\n\nDoctor: During this past year and seven months, was there ever a stretch when you clearly felt depressed nearly every day for two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I know you’re trying to get a clear yes or no. I just… I don’t feel confident saying it was clearly that. There were definitely some pretty low stretches, but I wasn’t keeping track day by day, and I don’t want to make it sound more definite than I can honestly say. So I guess my answer is: I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Has this been making it hard for you to function in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I’ve tried to keep it together, especially at work, but I’ve started missing things I really shouldn’t be missing—things that can affect deadlines, and honestly safety too. There was a near-miss that kind of shook me, and after that it was harder to tell myself it was just normal stress.\n\nAnd at home… I’m there, but I’m not really being the person they can count on like before. My wife and kids sort of have to work around me. I don’t like saying that, but… yeah, it’s been making daily life harder.\n\nDoctor: Thinking back over the past year and seven months, was there at least one period when you felt depressed nearly every day for two weeks straight?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to answer that the way you need it answered… but I still don’t feel totally sure. Looking back, there were definitely stretches where I was really low most of the time, but saying it was nearly every day for two straight weeks—I don’t know if I can honestly pin it down that clearly.\n\nI don’t want to dodge the question. I just… I’d have to say I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: During any 2-week period in the past year and seven months, did you feel sad, down, or hopeless most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I know I’m probably not giving you the clean answer you need. I just… I don’t know if I can say “yes” to that with confidence. There were times I felt really down, more than I’d like to admit, but whether it was most of the day nearly every day for a full two weeks… I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Aside from losing interest in things, have you actually been feeling sad or depressed?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess that’s the part I’m having trouble separating out. I wouldn’t say I’ve been walking around crying or anything like that. It’s more like… low, kind of heavy, not really myself.\n\nSo, sad or depressed? Maybe, yeah, at times. I just don’t know how strong to make that sound, if that makes sense. I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: When you’ve had a break from the situation at home, have you felt noticeably better?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve really noticed a clear difference. I mean, sometimes being away from the house is a little easier just because I’m not right in the middle of it, but I don’t know that I’d say I feel noticeably better.\n\nMaybe a little more distracted, if that counts? But not like everything lifts. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: If your wife were suddenly back to full health and things at home were normal again, do you think you would still feel this same loss of interest and heaviness?\n\nPatient: I’d like to think if she were better and things calmed down at home, I’d bounce back. I mean, that sounds like the right answer.\n\nBut… honestly, I’m not sure. The loss of interest feels like it’s gotten pretty broad. Like, my fishing gear is just sitting untouched in the garage, and I used to really look forward to that. My woodshop feels more like a chore now than something I enjoy. Even when my kids ask me to play chess, I’ll do it, but I’m kind of just going through the motions.\n\nSo… I don’t know. Maybe things being normal would help, but I’m not sure it would all just come back right away.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d use the word hopeless exactly. That feels… pretty strong.\n\nI guess there are moments where I think, “I don’t know how this gets better,” but then I tell myself that’s probably just me being tired or overwhelmed. I’m not sitting here thinking there’s no point or anything like that.\n\nDoes that make sense, or is that kind of what you mean by hopeless?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Yeah… some. I mean, I can still function, I don’t want to make it sound like I’m completely useless or anything.

But at work, it’s been harder than I’d like to admit. I used to be able to keep schedules and subcontractors straight in my head, and now I’ll read the same email two or three times and still not really take it in. In meetings I’ll lose my train of thought, forget what I was about to say, which is embarrassing.

And decisions… things I used to make in five minutes, I’ll kind of freeze up over now. I don’t know if that counts as concentration exactly, but it’s definitely not like me.

It’s been going on for about a year and seven months now. Around when my wife’s health problems started changing everything at home. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m blaming that, you know, but that’s about when I started noticing I wasn’t really myself.\n\nDoctor: For most of the past two years, have you felt down or depressed more days than not?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to judge “most,” honestly. I mean, I wouldn’t say I’m down every single day… but yeah, it’s been there a lot. More days than I’d like to admit, I guess. I’ve tried to just keep moving and not make a big thing of it, but it’s been pretty frequent.\n\nDoctor: Have these down feelings lasted continuously for at least two years?\n\nPatient: Not quite two years, no. I mean, it feels like it’s been a long time, but if I’m counting right it’s more like a year and seven months… since my wife’s health stuff started changing things at home. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: If your wife’s health problems had not happened, do you think you would still be feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know for sure… I’d like to think maybe I wouldn’t be. It really seemed to start when my wife’s health problems changed everything at home. That was about a year and seven months ago, and that’s when I started feeling like this. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel your reaction to your wife’s health problems has been stronger than most people would have in the same situation?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean, it’s a hard situation, so I don’t really know what a “normal” reaction would look like. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m handling it worse than someone else would… but it has hit me pretty hard. I guess I’m not the best judge of whether it’s more than most people.\n\nDoctor: During this time, have you felt depressed or down most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to measure “most of the day,” honestly. There have definitely been stretches where I’ve felt pretty down for a while, but I don’t want to overstate it or say it wrong. I guess I’d say it’s happened in periods, yeah… but I’m not sure if it meets exactly what you mean by nearly every day for two weeks.\n\nDoctor: Has there been a period of at least two weeks when you felt down or depressed nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I can say it that clearly. There have been times where it felt like I was down a lot of the time, maybe most days, but I’m not sure I tracked it well enough to say “yes, definitely two weeks nearly every day.” I don’t want to give you the wrong answer.\n\nDoctor: During this past year and seven months, was there ever a stretch when you clearly felt depressed nearly every day for two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I know you’re trying to get a clear yes or no. I just… I don’t feel confident saying it was clearly that. There were definitely some pretty low stretches, but I wasn’t keeping track day by day, and I don’t want to make it sound more definite than I can honestly say. So I guess my answer is: I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Has this been making it hard for you to function in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, I’ve tried to keep it together, especially at work, but I’ve started missing things I really shouldn’t be missing—things that can affect deadlines, and honestly safety too. There was a near-miss that kind of shook me, and after that it was harder to tell myself it was just normal stress.\n\nAnd at home… I’m there, but I’m not really being the person they can count on like before. My wife and kids sort of have to work around me. I don’t like saying that, but… yeah, it’s been making daily life harder.\n\nDoctor: Thinking back over the past year and seven months, was there at least one period when you felt depressed nearly every day for two weeks straight?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to answer that the way you need it answered… but I still don’t feel totally sure. Looking back, there were definitely stretches where I was really low most of the time, but saying it was nearly every day for two straight weeks—I don’t know if I can honestly pin it down that clearly.\n\nI don’t want to dodge the question. I just… I’d have to say I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: During any 2-week period in the past year and seven months, did you feel sad, down, or hopeless most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I know I’m probably not giving you the clean answer you need. I just… I don’t know if I can say “yes” to that with confidence. There were times I felt really down, more than I’d like to admit, but whether it was most of the day nearly every day for a full two weeks… I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Aside from losing interest in things, have you actually been feeling sad or depressed?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess that’s the part I’m having trouble separating out. I wouldn’t say I’ve been walking around crying or anything like that. It’s more like… low, kind of heavy, not really myself.\n\nSo, sad or depressed? Maybe, yeah, at times. I just don’t know how strong to make that sound, if that makes sense. I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: When you’ve had a break from the situation at home, have you felt noticeably better?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve really noticed a clear difference. I mean, sometimes being away from the house is a little easier just because I’m not right in the middle of it, but I don’t know that I’d say I feel noticeably better.\n\nMaybe a little more distracted, if that counts? But not like everything lifts. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: If your wife were suddenly back to full health and things at home were normal again, do you think you would still feel this same loss of interest and heaviness?\n\nPatient: I’d like to think if she were better and things calmed down at home, I’d bounce back. I mean, that sounds like the right answer.\n\nBut… honestly, I’m not sure. The loss of interest feels like it’s gotten pretty broad. Like, my fishing gear is just sitting untouched in the garage, and I used to really look forward to that. My woodshop feels more like a chore now than something I enjoy. Even when my kids ask me to play chess, I’ll do it, but I’m kind of just going through the motions.\n\nSo… I don’t know. Maybe things being normal would help, but I’m not sure it would all just come back right away.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d use the word hopeless exactly. That feels… pretty strong.\n\nI guess there are moments where I think, “I don’t know how this gets better,” but then I tell myself that’s probably just me being tired or overwhelmed. I’m not sitting here thinking there’s no point or anything like that.\n\nDoes that make sense, or is that kind of what you mean by hopeless?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: Yeah… some. I mean, I can still function, I don’t want to make it sound like I’m completely useless or anything.\n\nBut at work, it’s been harder than I’d like to admit. I used to be able to keep schedules and subcontractors straight in my head, and now I’ll read the same email two or three times and still not really take it in. In meetings I’ll lose my train of thought, forget what I was about to say, which is embarrassing.\n\nAnd decisions… things I used to make in five minutes, I’ll kind of freeze up over now. I don’t know if that counts as concentration exactly, but it’s definitely not like me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."

Yeah… sleep’s been pretty rough, I guess. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but I’ll get into bed completely exhausted and then just… not shut off.

I’ll lie there thinking about bills, schedules, everything I didn’t get done. Or I’ll fall asleep for a bit and then wake up around three or four in the morning, and after that I’m just staring at the ceiling until it’s time to drive to the site.

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… a little, I guess. I mean, I’m still eating some, so it’s not like I’ve stopped completely.

But most days food just doesn’t really taste like anything. Like cardboard, honestly. I’ll skip breakfast without really meaning to, and then at dinner I kind of just push food around or pick at it, even if I’ve been out on a job site all day.

I have noticed my belt’s had to come in a couple notches, but I wasn’t trying to lose weight or anything. Is that the kind of change you mean?

I mean, I wouldn’t say I’m down every single day… but yeah, it’s been there a lot. More days than I’d like to admit, I guess. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel your reaction to your wife’s health problems has been stronger than most people would have in the same situation?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean, it’s a hard situation, so I don’t really know what a “normal” reaction would look like. I don’t want to make it sound like I’m handling it worse than someone else would… but it has hit me pretty hard. I guess I’m not the best judge of whether it’s more than most people.\n\nDoctor: During this time, have you felt depressed or down most of the day nearly every day for at least two weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to measure “most of the day,” honestly. There have definitely been stretches where I’ve felt pretty down for a while, but I don’t want to overstate it or say it wrong. I guess I’d say it’s happened in periods, yeah… but I’m not sure if it meets exactly what you mean by nearly every day for two weeks.\n\nDoctor: Has there been a period of at least two weeks when you felt down or depressed nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I can say it that clearly. There have been times where it felt like I was down a lot of the time, maybe most days, but I’m not sure I tracked it well enough to say “yes, definitely two weeks nearly every day.” I don’t want to give you the wrong answer.\n\nDoctor: During this past year and seven months, was there ever a stretch when you clearly felt depressed nearly every day for two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I know you’re trying to get a clear yes or no. I just… I don’t feel confident saying it was clearly that. There were definitely some pretty low stretches, but I wasn’t keeping track day by day, and I don’t want to make it sound more definite than I can honestly say. I mean, I’ve tried to keep it together, especially at work, but I’ve started missing things I really shouldn’t be missing—things that can affect deadlines, and honestly safety too. There was a near-miss that kind of shook me, and after that it was harder to tell myself it was just normal stress.\n\nAnd at home… I’m there, but I’m not really being the person they can count on like before. My wife and kids sort of have to work around me. I don’t like saying that, but… yeah, it’s been making daily life harder.\n\nDoctor: Thinking back over the past year and seven months, was there at least one period when you felt depressed nearly every day for two weeks straight?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to answer that the way you need it answered… but I still don’t feel totally sure. I just… I’d have to say I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: During any 2-week period in the past year and seven months, did you feel sad, down, or hopeless most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I know I’m probably not giving you the clean answer you need. I just… I don’t know if I can say “yes” to that with confidence. There were times I felt really down, more than I’d like to admit, but whether it was most of the day nearly every day for a full two weeks… I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Aside from losing interest in things, have you actually been feeling sad or depressed?\n\nPatient: I mean… I guess that’s the part I’m having trouble separating out. I wouldn’t say I’ve been walking around crying or anything like that. It’s more like… low, kind of heavy, not really myself.\n\nSo, sad or depressed? Maybe, yeah, at times. I just don’t know how strong to make that sound, if that makes sense. I don’t want to overstate it.\n\nDoctor: When you’ve had a break from the situation at home, have you felt noticeably better?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve really noticed a clear difference. I mean, sometimes being away from the house is a little easier just because I’m not right in the middle of it, but I don’t know that I’d say I feel noticeably better.\n\nMaybe a little more distracted, if that counts? But not like everything lifts. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: If your wife were suddenly back to full health and things at home were normal again, do you think you would still feel this same loss of interest and heaviness?\n\nPatient: I’d like to think if she were better and things calmed down at home, I’d bounce back. Yeah… I have, though I keep telling myself it’s just work or getting older.
